## Plan Lineage

- Follow-up plans: None (PLAN.md Phases 4 and 5 — Cliniko API + embedded controls — are separate phases, not children of this plan)

## Goal
Make the scribe feel like less work than typing: transcribe WHILE the consultation is recorded so the note is ready when Stop is pressed; let the practitioner type their own shorthand over a note line (say "we'll put a crack into the neck" → the Treatment line becomes "HVLA Cx") and learn that as a rule from the edit, so the same utterance next time proposes the shorthand and, after three unchanged confirmations, lands pre-filled; offer four writing styles (Verbatim, Clean clinical, Own voice, Narrative — the Heidi-like prose) with the prose styles produced by a LOCAL language model that may only rephrase already-confirmed content and is checked afterwards; let the practitioner seed all of this by uploading one to five of their own past notes at first run or any time from settings, learned from and then deleted on their say-so; and replace the per-line click for the practitioner's own rules with visible marking plus Save as the single act of ratification.

## Planning Extraction Summary

**Workflow Schema:** v22

**Executor tier:** entirely premium — planned on Fable 5.1; executed in architect mode (Fable architect, `claude-opus-5` via the profile-level `loop-delegate` definition, as in practitioner-profile stages 3–6); tier-gap dosing applied (design decisions, edge-case inventory, data contracts, acceptance criteria and the caller manifest locked; no microcopy dictionary or permission matrix — single-user app). Tasks marked `[executor: premium-only]` are the custody, threading and checker work the architect keeps rather than delegates.

### Agreed Scope (Build Now)
- Live transcription during recording (Phase 1): a `LiveTranscriber` worker fed PLAINTEXT PCM by a tee installed before encryption, runs the existing stage functions one ~30 s window at a time (open segments force-closed at 30 s), keeps segments + per-segment embeddings in memory, never touches `SessionCrypto` or the store, never writes a file. Finish seals capture as today; the tail is drained on the processing thread, the shared `assemble_transcript` helper runs the speaker pass over the kept embeddings and `write_transcript` writes `transcript.enc` once. Discard stops the worker, drops its buffers and clears the live view. Any worker failure, queue overflow or fall-behind degrades to today's batch path with a named reason.
- Typed edits (Phase 2): the Note tab gains an Edit control on a note line or proposal that replaces its text with practitioner-typed wording — a `clinician` provenance assertion carrying the clinician's decision. This is the surface shorthand learning hangs off (the tab has no text editing today — code-verified).
- Expansion learning (Phase 2): on Save, a typed edit whose source utterance is the practitioner's own is recorded as a learned autofill rule (trigger → wording) appended to the practitioner's `autofill_rules.json` with a metadata sidecar, review-later on the Practitioner tab with one-click delete. Triggers pass THE refusal filter; typed wording passes the narrower `refuse_typed_wording` (numbers, dates, medications; practitioner decision 2026-09-18). After `LEARNED_RULE_AUTO_CONFIRM_AFTER = 3` unchanged confirmations the rule's lines arrive pre-filled; a Remove of a pre-filled line demotes the rule back to proposing (count reset), never deletes it.
- Save-as-ratification (Phase 2; practitioner decision 2026-09-18): lines from the practitioner's own `autofill_rules.json` / `prefill_templates.json` entries and from auto-confirmed learned rules enter the note with `ConfirmationDecision.decided_by = "config"` (config digest, confirmation count), rendered with a distinct "pre-filled" mark, removable, and ratified by Save whose label reads "Save — confirms the N pre-filled lines shown". Model-derived proposals keep per-line confirm. Interim review stays in the desktop Note tab until PLAN.md Phase 4 places the draft in Cliniko.
- Writing styles (Phases 3–4): a persisted `note_style` — `verbatim` (today's rendering), `clean` (terse one-line-per-assertion rendering, no substitution, no model), `own_voice` (local model conditioned on the style profile), `narrative` (local model, fuller standard clinical prose — the Heidi-like option). Default after this plan: `clean`. Prose styles run in the Note tab after finalisation, rephrase ONLY confirmed assertion text, pass the new fidelity gate (Check 5) and are ratified by Save; a failing section keeps `clean` with a review warning; one rendering path serves display, persistence and Copy.
- Sample-note learning (Phase 3): optional at first run (banner line, never blocking, no voice profile required — consent recorded in the style store) and always available on the Practitioner tab: 1–5 past notes per run (recommended 3–5), plain text, pasted text or `.docx`, read into memory only. Derived and saved to a DPAPI-wrapped `style_profile.json` under its own key: section order/heading map, shorthand from a shipped controlled vocabulary (unrecognised tokens tick-to-keep), style measures, and up to 30 exemplar sentences that pass the refusal filter UNCHANGED (practitioner decision 2026-09-18: refuse, never scrub), each shown for review with per-item remove before and after save; a key-first "Delete learned style" action. Deleting the originals is a separate explicit confirmation naming the paths, unticked by default.
- Local language model (Phase 4): the runtime chosen by Task 4.0's preflight (`llama-cpp-python` prebuilt CPU wheel or `onnxruntime-genai`), installed only as a pinned hashed wheel under `[ml]`; ONE ~4B instruct model at 4-bit (practitioner decision 2026-09-18: pin the 4B now) pinned in `scripts/setup-models.py` by SHA-256 with size and free-space checks; the no-sockets integration test is the offline control for this runtime.
- Consent v3 (Phase 0): covers typed-edit learning, sample-note learning and Save-as-ratification; v2 kept as history; re-consent on the tab.
- Note schema v2 (Phase 0): `note.enc` gains the new fields with defaults so v1 notes remain readable.
- Security docs, design system, retention schedule and threat model extended for every new surface (Phases 0 and H).

### Excluded — Revisit Only If Needed
- Training or fine-tuning any model on notes, transcripts or phrases
  - Why excluded: practitioner decision 2026-09-05 (parent plan). Sample-note learning is EXTRACTION into config; style conditioning is prompt-time only.
  - When to revisit: never for clinical data.
  - Relevant files / subsystems: `sample_notes.py` (new), the Phase 4 provider.
- Learning from patient or other-speaker lines; negative learning from removals — unchanged parent exclusions; a Remove of a pre-filled line demotes a learned rule to proposing but never deletes it.
- Any cloud or network path — unchanged; the model download stays inside `scripts/setup-models.py`.
- Scrubbing (editing) sentences to keep them as exemplars
  - Why excluded: practitioner decision 2026-09-18 — a control that edits instead of refusing breaks the fail-closed rule (`docs/lessons.md`); exemplars are kept only when they pass unchanged.
  - When to revisit: never.
- ~~Reading `.pdf` sample notes~~ — BUILT 2026-09-20 (Phase 4 live smoke, batch 2): the revisit trigger was met by the primary user — Cliniko exports notes as PDF ("cliniko saves notes as pdf, so make it so it can read pdf not .txt files only") — and the practitioner authorised `pypdf` (text extraction only, NO OCR: a scanned PDF is refused by name with the paste hint). See Task 3.3's AS-BUILT (the `.pdf` reader) and threat-model surface 16.
  - Was excluded because: an OCR/pdf dependency for little gain; the practitioner could paste text. OCR itself stays excluded.
- Learning trigger → wording from a typed line with NO matching practitioner utterance
  - Why excluded: no trigger exists to learn; the line is still added, just not learned.
  - When to revisit: never — definitional.
- Shorthand substitution at render time (the first draft's `clean` renderer)
  - Why excluded: it would change confirmed text AFTER the check and the digest; shorthand reaches a note only through a learned or authored rule the practitioner confirmed.
  - When to revisit: never.

### Accepted Assumptions — Revalidate Later
- One practitioner per Windows login (parent plan assumption). Trigger: commercialisation.
- Whisper `small` transcribes a ~30 s window in well under 30 s on the practitioner's machine (Phase 2 benchmark figures), so the live worker keeps up.
  - Why accepted: not a decision — the worker's fall-behind cut-off (D2) makes the failure mode a named degradation, not a correctness issue.
  - Risk if false: live mode switches itself off and Finish uses the batch path with the reason line; verified by Task 1.6's benchmark line.
- The 4B instruct model produces per-section prose that passes Check 5 in at least 9 of 10 fixture notes after prompt tuning.
  - Risk if false: the `gpt-oss-20b` deferral trigger fires.
- Exemplars that pass the refusal filter unchanged carry no patient identifier.
  - Why accepted: the filter refuses name-shaped, numeric, date and medication tokens; the practitioner reviews each exemplar; one-click delete.
  - Risk if false: a patient detail persists in `style_profile.json` (DPAPI-wrapped under its own key) — bounded by review and delete. Trigger: H3 security review.

### Key Design Decisions
See `## Design Decisions` D1–D12 (state-once). The ones that constrain everything else: the live worker sees plaintext PCM before encryption and never holds session crypto (D1); the model only ever sees confirmed assertion text, never the transcript (D6); no new provenance for config-decided lines — the decision carries `decided_by` (D4); sample notes are never copied and deletion of the originals is an explicit confirmation (D9).

### Codebase Integration Notes
- Live transcription reuses every stage of `transcribe_session` as functions; the worker is a driver over plaintext PCM, not a second pipeline and not a store reader. The recovery runner and the batch signature stay exactly as they are.
- `SessionController.transcribe` needs NO signature change: `build_transcriber` closes over the sealed worker, drains its tail and runs the document assembly + `write_transcript` inside the same `transcriber(directory, crypto)` callable on the processing `TaskThread` (the round-32/42 reservation reasoning in `transcribe()` stays literally untouched).
- `label_speakers` (`transcription.py:726`) takes raw PCM and is documented as PAIRED with the inline per-window path in `transcribe_session` (`:740`); the batch tail at `:1238–1250` decides from `np`, `saw_empty_segment`, `len(embeddings)`, `has_text`, `similarities` and `attributing`. The live path cannot call `label_speakers` (D1 drops PCM) — Task 1.0 extracts that tail into a shared `assemble_transcript(...)` helper used by both drivers (codex PR-MED-005), the public batch signature unchanged.
- Learning-on-Save is the single write point for learned cues; learned rules use the same moment, the same `known` pre-write duplicate check and the same user-file + metadata-sidecar shape.
- Config loading is whole-file with loud failure; a learned rule is validated as an `AutofillRule` BEFORE the write so a bad write cannot brick loading.
- `ui.models` is the composition layer: every new factory (live transcriber, typed-wording filter, style renderer, prose provider, sample-note learner) is built there so the screens stay ML-free and testable with mocks.
- Shared helpers across phases: `refuse_typed_wording` (Phase 2 typed wording ONLY — sample-derived shorthand uses the controlled vocabulary and exemplars the full refusal filter, D10), the learned-entry metadata sidecar model (cues today, rules in Phase 2), `model_file_report_lines` for the language model, the DPAPI-wrapped-store pattern (voice today, style profile in Phase 0).
- Gotchas (`docs/lessons.md`): model downloads must be run by the practitioner from a normal terminal (MSIX-virtualised agent shells); the app must never be launched from an ephemeral terminal; heredocs over ~1 KB fail in the Bash tool; verify=composer on this host; a control must fail toward safety (refuse, never edit-and-keep).

### External / API Findings
- Prose runtime installation: see D8 and Task 4.0 — `llama-cpp-python`'s PyPI default BUILDS from source and its prebuilt CPU wheels live on a separate index; `onnxruntime-genai` ships PyPI wheels; Python 3.14 availability of either is unverified until the practitioner's preflight. Neither has stubs (mypy override) nor a `local_files_only` switch; both load a local path only.
- Candidate 4B model: an instruct GGUF at Q4_K_M ≈ 2.5 GB (exact repository, file and SHA-256 recorded by Task 4.1 when pinned; Qwen3-4B-Instruct is the first candidate to try).
- `python-docx` has no stubs: mypy override needed; it joins BASE `dependencies` (the installer ships it), not `[ml]`.

## Planned Workflow Summary

### Flow 1 — A consultation with live transcription
- Start → the capture sink encrypts chunks to the store as today AND hands the same PCM to the live worker's queue. Each ~30 s of VAD-segmented audio is transcribed and embedded in memory and its segments posted to the Transcript screen (display-only, header "Live — updates while recording"). Pause stops feeding; Resume continues. Finish → capture flushes and the store is sealed (fast, on the GUI thread as today); the processing thread then drains the worker's tail, assembles the document with the speaker pass over the kept embeddings and writes it once; generation starts. If the worker died or fell behind, Finish runs the batch path with the status line "live transcription stopped; transcribing after the recording instead". Discard → the worker is stopped and its buffers dropped before the key is destroyed; the live view is cleared.

### Flow 2 — Learning shorthand from a typed edit
- The transcript holds the practitioner's line "ok we'll put a crack into the neck now"; the extractive draft routes it to Treatment. The practitioner presses Edit on that line, types "HVLA Cx", and saves. Save records `trigger="crack into the neck"` → `["HVLA Cx"]`, section `treatment`, appended to their `autofill_rules.json` with metadata in the sidecar (the trigger passed the refusal filter; the wording passed `refuse_typed_wording`). Next consultation the utterance yields a proposal "HVLA Cx" marked learned; after three unchanged confirmations it lands pre-filled, marked, and Save ratifies it.

### Flow 3 — Choosing a style
- Practitioner tab → Writing style: Verbatim / Clean clinical / Own voice / Narrative. Clean needs no model. Own voice and Narrative are greyed with a reason line when the language model is absent, and Own voice also when the style profile is empty.

### Flow 4 — Seeding from past notes
- First run shows a second banner line: "Optional: teach the scribe your note style from 1–5 past notes." The tab's "Learn from my notes" group accepts up to five files or pasted text, shows what will be kept (headings map, shorthand list, the exemplar sentences that passed unchanged) for review with per-item remove, saves on confirm, then asks separately whether to delete the listed original files.

## Design Decisions
- D1 — The live worker is fed plaintext PCM by a tee wrapped around the capture sink BEFORE encryption; it never holds `SessionCrypto`, never reads the store, never writes a file; it keeps per-window segments and per-segment embeddings (and enrolment cosines when a profile is applied) in memory and drops PCM per window. Finish assembles and writes once through the batch stage functions. Why: no custody entanglement, the idempotence and crash-recovery contracts untouched, the batch path stays the fallback. Rejected: a worker reading the encrypted store (custody under Discard); writing partial transcripts (breaks atomic replace and the footer contract).
- D2 — Lifecycle: the worker has its OWN handle on the live session (`_LiveSession.live_transcriber`), never the `transcribing` flag. `discard()` in RECORDING/PAUSED stops-and-joins it (bounded timeout) inside the first locked section before the key-first deletion and tells the Transcript screen to clear. `finish()` stays synchronous and cheap: it seals capture exactly as today (`worker.stop(flush=True)`, then `store.finish()`), marks the worker "sealed" (no more feeding) and leaves it attached to the live session; the TAIL DRAIN (the last open segment and any queued windows) happens on the existing processing `TaskThread` inside the transcriber callable (Task 1.3), never on the GUI thread (codex PR-MED-003: `ui/session_screen.py:175` calls `controller.finish()` directly in the GUI slot). From PROCESSING on, the worker is owned by that callable and the existing `transcribing` guard covers Discard as today; an exception in the drain marks live mode failed (batch fallback), never the session. `pause()`/`resume()` gate feeding. Cleanup is by ATTACHED-WORKER OWNERSHIP, not by state (codex PR-MED-014): whichever path leaves a worker attached — `_fail_locked` (a footer-write failure at `session.py:480–482` enters FAILED with no capture-failure callback), `_on_capture_failure`, retire-on-`start()`, and `discard()` in ANY state where the transcriber callable has not yet claimed the worker (RECORDING, PAUSED, FAILED, and the PROCESSING interval before `transcribing` is set at `:534`) — stops it, joins it and confirms its buffers are cleared BEFORE `discard_session` destroys the key; a join timeout is a reported failure, never counted as cleared; late live-view updates after a stop are dropped. The tee never raises into the sink: any error flips the worker to failed and is reported through the status line. Bounds (codex PR-MED-004): an open VAD segment is force-closed at `LIVE_MAX_SEGMENT_SECONDS = 30` at the lowest-probability frame of its last 5 s (the two halves are separate segments; `pack_transcription_windows` then never sees an oversized live segment); queued plaintext PCM is capped at three windows INCLUDING the model-load period — exceeding it, or more than two transcribed windows behind, stops the worker itself (failed, "live transcription could not keep up"). Why: the single-run-per-session `transcribing` guard (`session.py:513–535, 688–694`) would refuse Discard for the whole consultation if reused during RECORDING. Rejected: reusing the flag during recording; draining the tail inside `finish()`. AS BUILT (Task 1.1, 2026-09-19): the cut fires when the open span's PADDED length would reach the bound (so a half never exceeds 30 s even with its start pad); neither half is padded at the cut and the second never merges back; a second half shorter than `MIN_SPEECH_SECONDS` at the very end of the audio is dropped by the ordinary short-span filter (residue: ≤ 0.25 s of tail speech after a cut is absent from the LIVE document; the batch fallback keeps it). The drain yields to processing at the FIRST ready window (peer round 10 PR-LOW-022): a ready window is transcribed before more chunks are ingested and the queue is re-drained after every window, so the retained plaintext is at most the window in progress plus the open span plus one chunk whatever the producer's pace; fall-behind in practice is the QUEUE CAP tripping in `feed` when a provider slower than the feed lets audio pile up (`fell_behind`), and the windows-behind rule (evaluated at every pop and after every ingested chunk — round 9 PR-MED-018) survives as a DEFENSIVE bound that can fire only when a single ingested chunk closes more than three windows. Both cut-offs honour the seal as REQUESTED (`_sealed`, set before the sentinel is enqueued — peer round 10 PR-LOW-023): once Finish sealed, the queued remainder is a finite tail already bounded by the queue cap at seal time and always drains in full. The sealed tail is bounded in AUDIO, not in windows: at most the queue cap's 90 s of PCM (`LIVE_QUEUE_CAP_BYTES`, three windows' WORTH) plus the window in progress, and the window COUNT that audio packs into depends on the gap structure (a window is a run of segments no more than 3 s apart, so short utterances 5 s apart make many small windows — peer round 9 PR-LOW-020). `pause()`/`resume()` gate feeding as a CONTRACT check: a chunk fed while paused is a broken control ordering (the capture worker's own pause barrier guarantees none arrives) and fails the worker toward the batch path rather than misaligning the timeline. Live-posted segments carry `LIVE_SPEAKER_PENDING` ("pending"), never a cluster label — the speaker pass runs only at the drain and the final document replaces every live segment (Task 1.4 renders it as no speaker). `stop()` returns whether the buffers are CONFIRMED cleared (inspected: no queued PCM, no retained PCM, no per-segment product, no undrained result); `False` on a join timeout, and the daemon worker clears them itself when its blocked call returns. `LiveResult.window_timings` (audio seconds, provider wall seconds per window) feeds Task 1.6's latency line. AS BUILT (Task 1.2, 2026-09-19): (1) the segmenter's held segment is ALSO held while an open span's padded start lies inside its end (the leg-b1 suite found 38 of 40 merging-configuration cases where a segment was released before the span that would merge into it closed; unreachable with the shipped parameters, where the minimum silence exceeds twice the padding — the determinism pin now holds under both configurations). (2) Discard's stop-and-join runs in its EXISTING unlocked window (beside `worker.stop(flush=False)`, under the custody reservation) rather than inside the first locked section: the live worker never calls back into the controller so both placements are deadlock-free, but the controller lock is the GUI's 500 ms state-poll lock and the join is bounded by up to 10 s; the plan's guarantee — buffers confirmed cleared BEFORE `discard_session` — holds identically, and `transcribe()` cannot claim the worker in that window (the reservation refuses it). (3) Two stop bounds and ONE rule (peer round 9 PR-MED-017, fail closed): in-lock stops wait 1 s, Discard's unlocked stop the full bound; an uncleared worker is logged (`live_transcriber_stop_timeout`, id + state), stays ATTACHED, and REFUSES every step that would destroy or drop custody next — Discard raises `SessionActivityError` BEFORE `discard_session` (the session is routed to FAILED from RECORDING / PAUSED / pre-claim PROCESSING because its capture worker is already stopped; key + chunks kept; QUEUED / FAILED stay as they are; the reservation is released; the next Discard retries the idempotent stop and succeeds once the worker cleared itself), the three Complete paths raise and stay QUEUED (the lease kept), and `start()` refuses to retire a session whose worker is uncleared (handle + in-memory crypto kept). The only caller allowed to ignore the verdict is `_fail_locked`, which destroys nothing. The key never goes while a worker may hold plaintext — the exception the previous text documented is gone. (4) The factory is settable after construction (`set_live_transcriber_factory`) because `app.main` constructs the controller before the window that owns the live view.
- D3 — The Whisper provider and VAD for the live worker are constructed on the worker's own thread at Start; a load failure flips live mode to failed with the reason line and the consultation continues; at Finish the worker's provider is released before the batch fallback constructs its own, so two models are never resident. Why: `build_transcriber` deliberately loads inside the worker call to keep the GUI thread free.
- D4 — No new provenance literal. `ConfirmationDecision` gains `decided_by: Literal["clinician","config"]` (default `clinician` so v1 notes read), `config_digest` for `config`, optional `confirmation_count`. Config-decided lines keep `autofill`/`prefill` provenance and the existing `proposal_id`/`shown_text_digest`/`config_digest` evidence, minted at the emitter. A typed edit is a new provenance `clinician` (typed text, `decided_by="clinician"`, the replaced assertion's id recorded as `replaces`). Why: a fourth literal for config lines would force every provenance branch (`note.py` 332–348, ~395, ~1815, 1976–1979; `note_check.py` ×5; `session_store.py:817`; `ui/models.py`, `ui/note.py`) to learn a value that changes nothing about the text's origin; `clinician` is genuinely new (text typed, not quoted) and the branches must learn it once. Rejected: auto-clicking confirm in the UI (evidence would lie about who confirmed); `authored_rule`. Build addendum (Task 0.2, 2026-09-19): admitting `clinician` lines into the draft BASE (the working draft's additions) means `NoteDraft`'s validator can no longer be the provider-boundary control (a provider could return a fabricated typed line with complete evidence), so the round-28 confinement moved to `compose_draft`'s ingestion of the provider's output (`_confine_provider_output`, typed `ProviderOutputError`, transcript-only); the draft type and `finalise_note` keep refusing rule-authored base lines and require a clinician decision on every typed one.
- D5 — Save-as-ratification (practitioner decision 2026-09-18): pre-filled lines carry a distinct mark and the Save button reads "Save — confirms the N pre-filled lines shown"; the threat model's boundary-2 control moves from per-assertion click to visible marking plus one Save per note, and Save stays refused while any MODEL-derived proposal is pending. Check 3's `clinician_asserted` review warning (`note_check.provenance_warnings`, `note_check.py:1325–1330` at `b3a65a2`; "Check 4" here was a slip corrected at Task 0.5 — Check 4 is omission; today emitted for EVERY non-transcript assertion and gating Save at `ui/note.py:1074`) is NOT emitted for `decided_by="config"` assertions — Save is their acknowledgement; every other review warning keeps its gate (codex PR-MED-010). Auto-confirm after `LEARNED_RULE_AUTO_CONFIRM_AFTER = 3` unchanged confirmations; "unchanged" after auto-confirm means the pre-filled line was present and unedited at Save; a Remove of a pre-filled line resets the rule to proposing with count 0; an Edit of a learned rule's line REPLACES that rule's wording in place (same `rule_id`, same trigger, count 0, `auto_confirmed = False`, the previous wording kept in the sidecar's history) — exactly one active expansion per normalised trigger, which is what the loader enforces at `note_config.py:852–856` (codex PR-MED-007). Rejected: time-based thresholds; a cap on pre-filled lines (the practitioner authored every one); learning a corrected wording as a second rule under the same trigger. AS BUILT (Phase 2, 2026-09-19): (1) the emitter mints ``ProposalResolution``s, not assertions — ``note_fill.config_decisions`` — carried on ``NoteDraft.config_decisions`` and validated there; ``finalise_note`` accepts a config decision ONLY when it is field-for-field one the draft carries, so the review surface can pass a config decision on or replace it with the clinician's decline (Remove), never mint one; the base-confinement story of Task 0.2 is untouched. (2) "Practitioner-authored entries" = every autofill rule and prefill seed in the resolved config that is not a learned rule (the shipped defaults ship EMPTY, so every entry is the practitioner's), plus a learned rule whose sidecar record says ``auto_confirmed``; a learned rule below the threshold keeps its per-line row — that row is what "Save refused with a pending proposal" gates on today (no model provider emits proposals yet). (3) Remove of a pre-filled line = a clinician ``declined`` resolution recorded at Save (the line leaves the note at once); a declined or Removed or Edited learned line counts ``removed`` (count 0), a clicked confirmation or an unedited pre-filled line counts ``confirmed``, and ``removed`` wins for a rule with lines in both states; ``record_rule_outcomes`` is a BATCH (one sidecar write per Save). (4) The wording replacement writes the SIDECAR (count reset) before the rules file, so a failed rules write leaves the old wording needing its confirmations again — never a new wording that pre-fills unconfirmed. (5) The Save label reads "Save - confirms the N pre-filled line(s) shown" with the app's ASCII hyphen; plain "Save note" with none standing or after Save. (6) A malformed or unreadable sidecar at generation is read fail-SAFE (every learned rule proposes; a one-line note on the Note tab's config report), while the config itself still loads loudly. (7) Round 12: at Save the counts are recorded BEFORE new rules and in-place corrections (a corrected wording always starts at 0); with the learning status OFF at Save nothing new is learned and nothing is promoted, but a Remove still demotes (`removed` outcomes are recorded whatever the consent state — the practitioner's own act on the line, and the safe direction).
- D6 — Prose provider input is ONLY confirmed assertion texts grouped by section plus the style profile (own_voice) or a fixed narrative instruction; never the transcript, never proposals. Output is parsed per section and passed through NEW Check 5 (fidelity, codex PR-HIGH-001 — token containment alone certifies nothing): (a) every input assertion's content tokens (normalised) appear in that section's prose; (b) the prose adds NO content token absent from the inputs other than tokens on a shipped connective allow-list (`config_defaults/prose_connectives.json`: articles, prepositions, conjunctions, auxiliaries, pronouns, "reports/notes/advised"-class verbs) — so "neck pain with paralysis" fails on `paralysis`; (c) no negation or polarity token (`no`, `not`, `never`, `denies`, `without`, `nil`, `n't`) may be added or removed relative to the inputs — so "no neck pain" fails; (d) no number, date, medication or laterality token may be added, removed or changed. A section that fails keeps the `clean` rendering with a `style_fallback` review warning. Check 5 is a GATE, not a certificate: prose that passes is still shown to the practitioner and ratified by the same Save that ratifies pre-filled lines (D5) — Save is unavailable while prose is being rendered and only a displayed rendering can be saved (D7) — and the `clean` rendering of the same assertions is stored beside it as the evidential base. Why: Check 1 rebuilds byte-for-byte from coordinates and cannot validate a rephrase; unrestricted rephrasing cannot be mechanically certified, so the practitioner's reading is the control and Check 5 bounds what reaches their eyes. Rejected: generating the whole note from the transcript (the original 3B shape); containment-only checking. AS BUILT (Tasks 4.2–4.3, 2026-09-20): (1) rule (a) requires every FACT-BEARING input token — an input token that is itself on the connective allow-list (glue: "reports", "for", "the") may be dropped or re-tensed, because the same list lets the model add it; no polarity or protected token is a connective (pinned), so no negation, number, date, medication or side can leave through the exemption; (2) rules (c) and (d) compare MULTISETS (a second negation dropped, a number still present but its count changed, a side swapped in count all fail), rule (a) is set containment (a fact stated twice may be stated once) and rule (b) set membership; tokens are `note.content_tokens` (the one tokenisation), so case and edge punctuation are not facts and a typographic apostrophe changed by the model fails (a) — the safe direction; (3) the first failing rule is the verdict, reported as a code (`FidelityVerdict.rule`) and never a token; (4) the prose stage makes ONE model call per section (the parse is per section; a re-render after an edit asks only for the changed section; seconds are per section) and judges every section at once through `fidelity_verdicts` / `fidelity_warnings`; a failed section's `StyleRendering` carries no prose and the section's input digest, so the same lines are never asked twice in one review — the warning is re-derived from that carriage wherever renderings are (re)bound (`note.attach_style_renderings`), so a note's warnings and renderings cannot disagree; (5) an unusable completion (empty, an unclosed `<think>`, an echoed prompt marker, over-long) is judged as empty prose and fails `missing_fact` — "nothing usable" and "unfaithful" are one path; a runtime error yields no rendering and a named reason line, retried at the next finalisation; (6) the NAMED RESIDUE, pinned as passing: the rules see tokens, not attachment or order — sides swapped between two anatomy words, a negation moved between clauses with the same count, two numbers traded, a reversed comparison; the practitioner's reading is the control and the laterality-parser lesson (rounds 48–58) is why no binding parser is attempted; (7) the instruction-shaped line: the instruction text says the lines are data, and the ENFORCING control is Check 5 — a model that obeys by REPLACING the line produces prose without the line's tokens, fails (a) and the section keeps `clean` (pinned with a scripted mock); the honest limit (codex round 22 PR-LOW-037): a completion that repeats the line AND obeys it keeps every token, adds only repetitions and passes the token gate — pinned as a known-passing case; the practitioner's reading before Save is the control for that shape; (8) PROMPT TUNING (leg e6, 2026-09-20, `prose_style.PROMPT_VERSION = "narrative-v2"`): the real model went 0/10 → 10/10 in both styles on the prompt alone — the exact-form rule, the named framing words, a verb-less worked example, a short prompt (it is re-evaluated per section because the runtime's state is cleared after each call) — with NO widening of `PROSE_CONNECTIVES` and no change to the rules; the consequence, stated: what passes is the confirmed lines restated as sentences in their own words, because the fluent rewrite a narrative reader might expect ("The patient presents with…") is exactly what rule (b) refuses, and that is the safe direction — the measured record is on Task 4.3. (9) PHASE H ROUNDS 24 + 28 (MED-004, SEC-001/002, 2026-09-25): the allow-list was NARROWED — the antonym function words on/off, before/after, over/under, since/until, in/out, up/down and `if` (`note_check._POLARITY_BEARING_TOKENS`, pinned off the list) and `as`, the lower-case form of the shipped abbreviation `AS` (ankylosing spondylitis — Check 5 folds case, so it was glue; `note_config._refuse_vocabulary_overlap` now refuses ANY such overlap at import as a broken install; 141 → 127 entries) reverse a clinical meaning when swapped or dropped and were free glue under (a)/(b), so "off paracetamol" → "on paracetamol" passed every rule; they are facts now (SET semantics like every fact: a dropped one fails (a), an added one (b), a merge of two lines sharing `on` still passes), the token-not-attachment residue keeps two such words swapped BETWEEN facts as a known-passing case, and the REAL model was re-measured on the ten fixtures under the narrowed list — after round 24 (130 words): narrative 10/10 (5.2 s per section mean, 6.1 max), own voice 10/10 (6.6 s, 7.3 max); after round 28 (127 words): narrative 10/10 (5.2, 6.1), own voice 10/10 (6.5, 7.0); after codex round 30 PR-MED-044 (118 words — the modals `should`/`shall`/`will`/`would` and the temporals `during`/`while`/`when`/`within`/`between` are facts too; `_POLARITY_BEARING_TOKENS` is 22 words): narrative 10/10 (5.0, 6.0), own voice 10/10 (6.4, 7.1) — no prompt change any time. The residue (6) is restated: no finite allow-list closes "an added or dropped connective shifts tense or adds an interval" — the auxiliaries (`has`/`had`/`is`/`was`…) stay glue because the tuned prompt's re-tensing relies on them, so "had surgery" → "has surgery" still passes; the practitioner's reading is the control.
- D7 — Styles are a rendering stage AFTER finalisation in the Note tab (codex PR-MED-002: `build_note_generator` returns an unchecked draft, `ui/models.py:1109`; `finalise_note` runs at `ui/note.py:1013`, and display and Copy both use `format_note_body`, `ui/note.py:1014, 1129`). `verbatim` and `clean` are deterministic (`clean` = one terse line per assertion, no substitution); the prose styles run on a `TaskThread` after each finalisation, bound to the finalised note's digest, invalidated by any subsequent edit, and wrap `clean` as their fallback. ONE rendering path: `format_note_body(note)` reads `GeneratedNote.style_renderings` (per-section prose + input digest + Check 5 verdict) and falls back to `clean` per section, for display, `note.enc` persistence/reload and Copy alike — a stale rendering (digest mismatch) is never shown. Setting stored in `practitioner_settings.json`, default `clean`. AS BUILT (Task 3.2, 2026-09-19): `note.render_note` lives in `note.py` beside the model (the labels and the D5 mark moved there; `ui.models` re-exports them); `clean` = the section title WITHOUT a colon, then each assertion's confirmed text on its own line — no bullet, no provenance tag (the tag is review apparatus the Note tab's line editor keeps per row) — and a pre-filled line keeps `  [pre-filled by your config]` because D5 requires the mark wherever the line is rendered; the binding is `section_input_digest` (the section's texts joined by newlines), recomputed by `usable_rendering` before any prose is shown, so a rendering survives only a section whose texts, order and count are exactly what it was given; the note's `style` is stamped in the Note tab at every re-finalisation from `read_note_style` (absent file → `clean`; unreadable → `clean` + a reason line), and a prose style chosen while no language model exists renders as `clean` with `style_fallback_line` in the tab's info label (C8) — no rendering is ever produced in Phase 3. AS BUILT (Task 4.4, 2026-09-20): the stage is ONE `TaskThread` job at a time per Note tab, started after every finalisation for a prose style and bound to `note_input_digest` of the note it started for; while it runs the style line under the note body says so and Save is disabled (the button AND a click-time refusal naming the reason); a result lands on the GUI thread, is bound through `attach_style_renderings` — only where a rendering's digest is its section's digest NOW — then DISPLAYED, then Save is re-enabled, so what Save snapshots is what was shown (PR-MED-015); an edit made while a job ran invalidates exactly the sections it changed and a fresh job asks the model for those only (the previous note's still-valid renderings are carried across every re-finalisation by `carry_renderings`); a job orphaned by `clear()` keeps its thread object until it ENDS — disposal rides the thread's own `finished` signal, never a timed join, and the tab stays `is_busy` (the window's close guard) while any rendering thread runs (codex round 22 PR-MED-036) — and its result is dropped; a model that cannot load, a missing learned style, a Check-5-refused section (kept as `clean` with the `style_fallback` review warning that gates Save until acknowledged) and an unexpected stage failure are each named on the same line (C8), and when such a reason lands on a note that still shows prose rendered earlier the line names the mixture instead of "this note is shown as Clean clinical" (`models.with_retained_prose`, codex round 22 PR-LOW-038); a section rendered as prose that holds a pre-filled line ends with a SECTION-level `[includes N lines pre-filled by your config]` mark (`note.prefilled_section_mark`, codex round 22 PR-MED-035 — D5's mark where the line itself no longer exists as a line; the editor rows still name the lines); the prose radios enable when `language_model_available()` is true.
- D8 — Language-model offline contract: the runtime is installed ONLY as a prebuilt CPU wheel pinned by version AND sha256 (`pip --require-hashes` from a documented wheel source; a source build is refused — codex PR-MED-006: the PyPI default of `llama-cpp-python` BUILDS from source and its prebuilt CPU wheels live on a separate index; Python 3.14 wheel availability is unverified); the runtime itself is chosen by Task 4.0's preflight `[decision]` between `llama-cpp-python` (prebuilt CPU wheel) and `onnxruntime-genai` (PyPI wheel, sits on the already-shipped onnxruntime, ONNX model export instead of GGUF); the wheel fetch is recorded in `data-flow-map.md` as the second sanctioned fetch beside `setup-models.py`. The model file is pinned by SHA-256 in `setup-models.py` with a size and free-space precondition and `.candidate` resume; loaded from the local path only; `assert_offline_env` still runs (it is the app-wide invariant) but the ENFORCING control for this runtime is the no-sockets integration test extended to a prose generation. Why: neither runtime reads a kill-switch variable; claiming parity with the Whisper contract would be the `docs/lessons.md` structure-does-not-enforce failure. AS BUILT (Task 4.1, 2026-09-20): (1) the runtime pin lives in `desktop/requirements-ml-prose.txt` (ONE hashed direct-URL requirement — the GitHub release asset — under `--require-hashes`) and NOT under `[ml]`: PyPI carries only an sdist of `llama-cpp-python`, so a `[dev,ml]` install (AGENTS.md step 2, CI's best-effort `[ml]` step) would BUILD it from source — exactly what this decision forbids — and pip cannot pin a hash or a wheel index inside `pyproject.toml`; the `[ml]` comment records this and points at the file; the install is `pip install --require-hashes --no-deps -r desktop\requirements-ml-prose.txt` (hash-checking mode demands a hash for every package it would download, and the runtime's two pure-Python dependencies not yet in the venv — `diskcache`, `jinja2` — are installed wheel-only in a second step, then `pip check`); (2) the gate test (`tests/test_language_model_runtime.py::TestInstalledRuntimeGate`) refuses an installed copy that lacks PEP 610 `direct_url.json` naming the pinned wheel URL and its verified sha256 — pip writes that record only for a URL install, so an index-resolved or source-built copy fails the gate (it skips by name where the runtime is absent, as in the executor's shell and on CI); stated exactly (codex round 22 PR-LOW-039): the hashed install verifies the downloaded ARCHIVE, the gate audits the install RECORD and catches a wrong install route — neither measures the installed bytes, and a forged record over locally built bytes is inside the same-user boundary; (2a) the runtime's `LLAMA_CPP_LIB_PATH` native-library override is refused at the offline contract (codex round 22 PR-MED-033): `apply_offline_env` deletes it, `assert_offline_env` refuses it by name without reading it as a path (`benchmark.FORBIDDEN_NATIVE_OVERRIDES`); `CUDA_PATH` / `HIP_PATH` are residue, not refused; (2b) the resident model's inference state — token buffers and KV cache — is cleared at the end of every `complete` (`LocalLanguageModel._clear_inference_state`, codex round 22 PR-MED-034), weights untouched, so a prompt and completion live for one call; (3) `LocalLanguageModel` checks the file SIZE against the pin before the digest (a fast refusal for a partial or wrong file) and runs a smoke completion at construction; the model is resident ONCE per process (`ui/models._LanguageModelCache`, built on the first stage job's worker thread), a failed load is remembered for the process and named on screen; (4) `ui/models.language_model_available()` is a `find_spec` of the runtime AND a stat of the pinned file — never a load or a decrypt, so the Practitioner tab's poll may ask it; (5) the enforcing offline control is `tests/test_integration_no_sockets.py`'s two prose legs (mock: polled while provably inside a model call, always; real: continuous polls across the load and one generation, skip-by-name until Task P.1); the setup entry streams the 2.33 GiB file with Range resume, a free-space precondition and every read bounded by the pin — a declared Content-Length is checked but never trusted as an allocation bound.
- D9 — Sample notes are read into memory from the chosen files and never copied; the learning output is `style_profile.json` DPAPI-wrapped under its OWN root `style\` with its own `key.dpapi` and a distinct verified description (voice and style keys cannot open each other's store, nor can a session key); deleting the originals is a separate explicit confirmation naming the paths, default unticked. The style store carries its OWN `ConsentRecord` (version + timestamp + `learning_opt_in`), written at sample Save from the tab's consent box, so sample learning at first run needs no voice profile (codex PR-MED-008: today consent persists only inside `voice.enc`, `ui/practitioner.py:588–590`, and `learning_status` refuses without a profile, `ui/models.py:1058`); `consent_is_current` accepts either store's record, and a stale record on either side re-asks on the tab. The style store has its own visible "Delete learned style" action (key-first, independent of the voice profile, which `delete_profile` at `ui/practitioner.py:762` does not touch — codex PR-MED-009) and a post-save "Learned style" list with per-exemplar delete. Rejected: a second artefact under `profile\` (would overwrite or share the voice key); auto-delete; requiring enrolment before sample learning. AS BUILT (Task 3.3, 2026-09-19): `sample_notes.read_sample_note` reads a `.txt` (one byte read; utf-8-sig, then cp1252) or a `.docx` (python-docx `Document` — a read of the package; paragraphs, then table cells) ONCE into `SampleNote.sample_text` (repr-hidden, a tripwire marker), bounded at 2 MiB and 200k characters; pasted text carries no path; `delete_sample_files` unlinks EXACTLY the listed paths (a directory is refused, never walked; a file already gone counts as deleted; other failures are reported per path) and is reached only through the tab's separate confirmation (`ui/style_review.DeleteOriginalsDialog`: the paths listed, "Delete these files now" unticked, `delete_requested()` true only when accepted AND ticked). AS BUILT (Tasks 3.4–3.6, 2026-09-19): the consent gate is the tab's EXISTING consent box under the v3 text (the v3 text covers the learned style by data class; the box's label still names the voice fingerprint — the group's own gate line says no voice profile is needed) and it is pre-ticked from the VOICE record only (PR-HIGH-006): a style record alone never pre-ticks it, so after a restart with no voice profile the practitioner ticks again to learn again — a click, not a leak; the style store's `ConsentRecord` is minted at the Save from that tick and the tab's learning box; the learn runs on the GUI thread (1–5 small text files; the review is modal anyway); the tab holds the decrypted profile in memory between its own events (the exemplars in the "Learned style" list are the practitioner's own sentences, the same class as the learned-phrase lists) and drops it on Delete; since peer round 17 (PR-MED-019) the two Flow-4 dialogs are disposed on EVERY exit of their runners — the answer read, then `release()` (the draft, the removed-index set and every row dropped; `choice()` refuses afterwards), `setParent(None)`, `deleteLater()` — and the tab drops the draft itself right after `build_style_profile`; the plaintext has THREE lifetimes (round 18 PR-LOW-029): the dialog's draft and rows (released on every runner exit), the handler's own copies (the read texts dropped after the learner, the draft and choice after the profile is built, the pasted source string held until `on_learn_from_notes` returns — through the save and the delete-originals dialog) and the paste box's text (kept on Cancel or a failed Save for retry, cleared only by a successful Save); the derived, reviewed profile is the only thing persisted. The `.docx` reader's memory bound is its OWN (round 17 PR-MED-020 → round 18 PR-LOW-027): the central directory is a fast first refusal (≤ 512 members, ≤ 8 MiB declared per member, ≤ 16 MiB declared in total, STORED / DEFLATED only), then this module inflates every member itself through `ZipFile.open(...).read(cap + 1)` — never an unbounded read; the stdlib rounds the request to its 4 KiB floor and passes it as `max_length`, so the decompressor's allowance per call is `max(cap + 1, 4096)` bytes (8 MiB + 1 in production), the RETURNED bytes are bounded by the declared size and CRC and the total by the accumulated-payload check (round 19 PR-LOW-031); an encrypted part is refused by its flag bit at the directory stage and corrupt DEFLATE data by name around the read (PR-LOW-030) — and re-zips the bounded bytes STORED into a `BytesIO` that python-docx parses instead of the chosen file; the text is bounded while collected; the stdlib's four metadata raises (`BadZipFile`, `OSError`, `UnicodeDecodeError`, `NotImplementedError` — PR-LOW-028) are named at that boundary, never a bare `except`.
- D10 — Exemplars: max 30; a sentence is kept only if it passes THE refusal filter UNCHANGED (no name-shaped, numeric, date or medication token) — refused sentences are dropped, never edited; each kept exemplar is shown and individually removable before save and after (D9). Sample-derived SHORTHAND is auto-extracted from patient notes, so it does NOT get the typed-wording filter (codex PR-MED-012): a token is admitted only if it is in the shipped controlled vocabulary `config_defaults/clinical_abbreviations.json` (the common physio/osteo/chiro abbreviations — HVLA, Cx, Tx, Lx, ROM, NAD, Rx, Hx …); an unrecognised all-caps or abbreviation-shaped token is listed separately as "unrecognised — tick to keep" (default unticked) and only a ticked one is saved. The retention schedule and non-flow list gain a row stating that exemplars are practitioner-reviewed sentences from the practitioner's own past notes under the style key, deleted with that store. AS BUILT (Task 3.3, 2026-09-19): an exemplar candidate is a sentence of at least 3 words (split on terminal punctuation or a line break) under a matched heading; each is passed to `refuse_learning_candidate(sentence.split(), first_in_segment=True)` UNCHANGED and dropped whole on any class; the learner passes ONE extra, `known_common` — every word that appears in lowercase anywhere in the chosen notes (`sample_notes._evidence`, punctuation-stripped by `note.strip_token_punctuation`) — and the filter's Task 3.7 admissions apply: a shipped abbreviation is never name-like at any position (rule (a), exact case-preserving match) and a capitalised REAL-first word is admitted when its lowercase form is in the evidence (rule (b)); every other capitalised opener ("Pt reports …", "Seen …", "Review …" when never written lowercase) and every capitalised mid-sentence word outside the vocabulary is still refused — the remaining narrowing, the safe direction; a sentence before any heading is counted as left out; at most 30 are taken ROUND-ROBIN across the sections in order of appearance so one long section cannot crowd the others out. Shorthand: `recognised` = EXACT, case-preserving membership of `CLINICAL_ABBREVIATIONS` (`as` is a word, `AS` the abbreviation; `hvla` in lowercase is neither recognised nor abbreviation-shaped); `unrecognised` = an all-capitals token of 2–8 letters, a `Cx`-form (`^[A-Z]{1,4}x$`) or a `C/O`-form, never a digit-bearing token, at most 100 listed; `build_style_profile` writes the recognised tokens plus the ticked ones and refuses any token or exemplar the draft did not offer (the review may only remove).
- D11 — `refuse_typed_wording(text)` is a second, narrower filter for PRACTITIONER-TYPED text ONLY: refuses numbers, dates and medication tokens (sharing the token classifiers with `refuse_learning_candidate`), no name heuristic (practitioner decision 2026-09-18). Triggers are still derived from transcript words through `refuse_learning_candidate` with `first_in_segment`/`following` supplied for the utterance position, trigger ≤ 6 content tokens. Why: the transcript filter refuses "HVLA Cx" as a name; typed wording is the practitioner's own, shown on screen and named at Save. AS BUILT (Phase 2, 2026-09-19): the trigger is the utterance's LAST ≤ 6 content tokens after trailing discourse tokens are dropped (``propose_rule_trigger``; at least ``LEARNED_PHRASE_MIN_TOKENS`` = 2 — one token would fire on almost every consultation), because clinical speech carries its discourse at the front and the router matches a contiguous run anywhere in a segment — Flow 2's "ok we'll put a crack into the neck now" learns "put a crack into the neck"; a heuristic on WHAT is learned, never a control: THE refusal filter admits the words (with the candidate's real position context) and the review-later list with delete is the control over what stays. The note line's own text is admitted by ``ui.models.check_typed_text`` (non-blank, bounded, the one config-text control-character validator) and by neither refusal filter — they decide what is LEARNED, not what the clinician may write; a typed edit over another speaker's line or over a hand-authored config line teaches nothing and says so; over a learned rule's own line it queues that rule's in-place wording replacement. Candidates are derived at the EDIT (so the status names the trigger and wording at once) and written only on Save.
- D12 — Consent v3 is REWRITTEN by data class, not appended (codex PR-MED-011: v2 promises "refuses phrases containing names, numbers, dates or medication names" and "Nothing else about any patient is stored beyond their session", `ui/models.py:1262–1264`, both of which this plan changes): the voice fingerprint; learned phrases and learned rules (transcript-derived triggers refused for names/numbers/dates/medications; typed wording refused for numbers/dates/medications only — the practitioner is responsible for names in their own typed shorthand); the learned style (what is derived from uploaded notes, that exemplar sentences are kept only when they pass the filter unchanged and are shown for review, stored encrypted, deletable); Save ratifying pre-filled lines. `CONSENT_TEXT_V2` is frozen as a literal historical text with its own literal "Version consent-v2." (today it interpolates the mutable `CONSENT_TEXT_VERSION`, `ui/models.py:1267`). The complete v3 text is pinned verbatim in a test beside its version.

## Schema / Data Changes
- `autofill_rules.json` (practitioner's user file): learned rules appended as ordinary `AutofillRule`s with `rule_id = "learned-<ulid>"`; a corrected wording replaces the rule's `expansion` in place (D5). `autofill_rules.learned.json` metadata sidecar (never read by the loader): `{schema_version: 1, entries: {rule_id: {learned_at, trigger_source: "typed_edit", confirmations: int, auto_confirmed: bool, history: [{replaced_at, previous_expansion}]}}}`. AS BUILT (Phase 2, 2026-09-19): `previous_expansion` is the replaced expansion's text LIST (a learned rule has one entry; a hand-edited one may have more); the ULID is 26 Crockford-base32 characters over a 48-bit millisecond stamp + 80 random bits, generated in `note_config` with no dependency; the whole-file replacement re-serialises the practitioner's rules file in the canonical `model_dump(mode="json")` form (hand comments cannot exist in JSON, so nothing is lost); the sidecar is read by the emitter (`load_learned_rule_entries`, fail-safe at generation) and by the Practitioner tab (`load_learned_rules`).
- `style_profile.json` (DPAPI-wrapped under `style\` with its own `key.dpapi`): `{schema_version: 1, learned_at, consent: ConsentRecord, section_order: [section_key], heading_labels: {section_key: str}, shorthand: [str], measures: {mean_sentence_words, abbreviation_ratio, person, tense}, exemplars: [{section_key, text}], source_count: int}`. `ConsentRecord` is the existing voice-profile consent model reused (version, timestamp, `learning_opt_in`). AS BUILT (Task 0.3, 2026-09-19): the ciphertext file is `style\style.enc` (the `.enc` convention names ciphertext; the shape above is its plaintext) beside its own `key.dpapi` (description `"ClinikoScribe practitioner style key"`, AAD `clinikoscribe-practitioner-style-v1`); the MODEL `StyleProfile` (+ `StyleMeasures`, `StyleExemplar`) lives in `note_config.py`, not `practitioner_profile.py`, because it names canonical section keys and `practitioner_profile` cannot import `note` (`transcription` imports `practitioner_profile`; the cycle broke at import) — the store functions in `practitioner_profile.py` import the model at call time; the exemplar field is `exemplar_text` (a tripwire signature); `shorthand` entries are one-word tokens (≤ 32 chars, ≤ 500); `measures.person` / `tense` are closed literals (`first|third|mixed|unknown`, `past|present|mixed|unknown`); ONE custody implementation (`_SealedStore` + `_seal` / `_open` / `_unlink_store`) serves the voice and style stores, and `ProfileUnusableError` carries a `store` label.
- `config_defaults/clinical_abbreviations.json` (shipped controlled vocabulary, D10) and `config_defaults/prose_connectives.json` (Check 5's allow-list, D6); both loaded with the same completeness guard as `section_cues.json`. AS BUILT: `{"schema_version": 1, "abbreviations": [...]}` → `note_config.CLINICAL_ABBREVIATIONS` (case-preserving) and `{"schema_version": 1, "connectives": [...]}` → `note_config.PROSE_CONNECTIVES` (lower-case); one-word entries, no duplicates, refused at import via `_parse_shipped_vocabulary` (`RuntimeError` "broken install"); package data only — never user files, never in the config digest.
- `practitioner_settings.json`: `{schema_version: 1, note_style: "verbatim|clean|own_voice|narrative"}`. AS BUILT: `note_config.PractitionerSettings` + `load_practitioner_settings` (absent = defaults, `clean`; unreadable / malformed typed) + `save_practitioner_settings` (atomic); NOT part of `NoteConfig` and NOT in the config digest — the note records its own `style`.
- `note.enc` schema v2: `ConfirmationDecision.decided_by` (default `clinician`), `config_digest`, `confirmation_count`; provenance literal `clinician` with `typed_text` and `replaces`; `GeneratedNote.style` (default `verbatim`) and `style_renderings: {section_key: {prose, input_digest, verdict}}` (D7); readers accept v1 and v2. AS BUILT (Task 0.2, 2026-09-19): the typed text IS the `clinician` span's `span_text` — no second `typed_text` field, so the shown digest and the carried text cannot name different words; `replaces` lives on `NoteAssertion` (clinician provenance only, never self); a `clinician` assertion has `proposal_id = None` and its decision's `proposal_id` names the assertion itself; a `config` decision's `config_digest` must equal the assertion's; `style_renderings` is a canonically-ordered tuple of `StyleRendering(section_key, prose_text, input_digest, verdict)` bound to populated sections (the module's tuple convention; `prose_text` is tripwire-distinctive and registered in `logging_setup`); a `failed` verdict carries no prose (C4); `schema_version` is `Literal[1, 2]` — an unknown version is refused and a note labelled 1 cannot carry v2 content; the `style_fallback` warning code is registered with its emitter (Task 4.2), because `WARNING_COPY` must name only affordances that exist.
- `TranscriptDocument`: unchanged.
- Consent: `CONSENT_TEXT_V3`, `CONSENT_TEXT_VERSION = "consent-v3"`.

## Config / Environment / Deployment Impact
- New runtime dependencies: `python-docx` (base, added in Phase 3 Task 3.3); the prose runtime chosen by Task 4.0 (`llama-cpp-python` prebuilt CPU wheel from its documented wheel index, or `onnxruntime-genai` from PyPI), installed ONLY as a pinned wheel with `--require-hashes` — never a source build (D8) — under `[ml]`; both libraries added to the mypy `ignore_missing_imports` override. The wheel fetch becomes the second sanctioned network step, documented beside `setup-models.py` in `data-flow-map.md` and `AGENTS.md` Local Run Steps.
- Python 3.14 compatibility of the chosen runtime is UNVERIFIED — Task 4.0's preflight on the practitioner's machine decides; if neither runtime ships a 3.14 CPU wheel the fallback recorded there is a 3.12 venv for the app (CI already runs 3.12).
- New pinned model entry in `scripts/setup-models.py` (~2.5 GB; practitioner-run from a normal terminal; free-space precondition; `.candidate` resume). The parent plan's installer decision covers this file. The retention schedule's models-cache size row is updated.
- The shipping-gate run config is unchanged; the gate runs under `clean` (Task H5 addendum).
- No env vars. Release risk: the default style flips to `clean` for the existing user; `verbatim` remains selectable.

## Critical Constraints
- C1 — No cloud, no network, no training (parent plan). Every model load is local-path + SHA-256 pin; the no-sockets test covers every ML runtime.
- C2 — The transcript remains raw evidence and display-only; the live view is the same widget under the same `NoTextInteraction` rule and is cleared on Discard.
- C3 — Nothing enters a saved note without a recorded decision: model-derived content keeps per-line confirm; config-decided lines carry `decided_by="config"` with digest evidence and are ratified by Save (D5); typed lines carry the clinician's decision. Copy stays behind `COPY_TO_CLINIKO_ENABLED`.
- C4 — The prose model never sees transcript text (D6); its output never bypasses Check 5, is never shown, persisted or copied from a stale rendering (D7), and is ratified by Save like every other non-transcript line.
- C5 — Three admission paths, one class of control: `refuse_learning_candidate` for transcript- or note-derived sentences (cue phrases, rule triggers, exemplars); `refuse_typed_wording` for practitioner-typed text (rule wording); the controlled vocabulary plus explicit tick-to-keep for auto-extracted shorthand (D10). All refuse; none edits.
- C6 — A learned or extracted artefact is written only on an explicit Save/Confirm and validated before the write; sample files are never copied; their deletion is a separate explicit confirmation (D9).
- C7 — Key custody: the live worker never holds session crypto (D1); no path destroys a session key while any worker holds that session's crypto; Discard drops the worker's plaintext buffers before the key is destroyed (D2).
- C8 — Every fallback names its reason on screen: live worker failed / could not keep up / model failed to load; language model absent; style profile empty; Check 5 failed.
- C9 — Nothing logs clinical text: live segments, typed wording, exemplars and prose never reach a log line.

## Validation / Verification
- Baseline (executed 2026-09-18 on `b3a65a2`): `desktop/` `ruff check .` clean, `mypy` clean over 34 files, `pytest` 2023 passed in 123 s; extension 36. Every phase closes at ruff clean, mypy clean, pytest green with the new tests added.
- Phase 0: a v1 `note.enc` fixture reads under v2; a `config` decision without a digest is unrepresentable; voice, style and session keys cannot open each other's store; a v2 consent record reads but is not current.
- Phase 1: `assemble_transcript` parity test — the batch path before and after extraction yields the same document for the windowed-batching fixtures, and live vs batch agree for empty/one-segment input, no profile, enrolled matches and textless clusters; VAD determinism pin (same frames → same segments) and the live document passes every invariant `read_transcript` enforces; forced segment close at 30 s of sustained speech (a 90 s continuous-speech fixture yields ≥ 3 segments and live updates); queued-PCM cap during a blocked model load → failed → batch; Discard during RECORDING stops and joins the worker, its buffers are gone and the view is cleared BEFORE `key.dpapi` is deleted (thread test); a tee exception never reaches `_fail()`; fall-behind → failed → batch fallback with the reason line; Finish stays under 100 ms with a blocked tail (GUI responsiveness) and the last flushed chunk is in the live tail drained on the TaskThread; `test_integration_no_sockets.py` extended to the live path.
- Phase 2: the Edit control produces a `clinician` assertion and every provenance branch handles it (a parametrised test over the branch list in Key Findings); `refuse_typed_wording` class test (accepts "HVLA Cx", refuses "paracetamol 500 mg", "3/4/26"); learned rule validated as `AutofillRule` and duplicate-checked BEFORE the write; a corrected wording replaces in place and reloads as one active rule per trigger (correction, reload, undo/cancel, write-failure cases); written only on Save (Cancel/Discard/Delete write nothing); auto-confirm at exactly three, Remove demotes; no `clinician_asserted` warning for `decided_by="config"` lines and an end-to-end case where a note holding only pre-filled lines is ratified by the counted Save while an unrelated review warning still gates; Save is refused with a pending model proposal.
- Phase 3: learner never writes to or copies from the source directory; an exemplar with any refused token is dropped unchanged; an unrecognised abbreviation is saved only when ticked; sample Save with NO voice profile records consent in the style store and `consent_is_current` reads it; "Delete learned style" is key-first and works without a voice profile; the delete-originals step touches only the listed paths and only after confirmation; style setting round-trips; first-run banner appears once; the 5 s microphone poll never decrypts the style store.
- Phase 4: the runtime installs only from the pinned hashed wheel (an install/import/smoke gate refuses a source build); pinned model loads from the local path only; Check 5 class test — missing fact, added content token ("with paralysis"), added negation ("no neck pain"), removed negation, added number/medication/laterality each FAIL; a faithful rephrase passes; failure falls back to `clean` for that section with the warning; a stale rendering (digest mismatch after an edit) is never displayed, persisted or copied; Save is disabled during a blocked render and a render that completes cannot persist unseen wording (blocked-render/Save test); display, reload and Copy produce identical text; the prompt builder is pinned never to accept `TranscriptDocument` and an instruction-shaped assertion is rendered, not obeyed; no-sockets test covers a prose generation; fixture pass rate recorded (acceptance ≥ 9/10).
- Manual (practitioner): live transcript filling during a real recording; Flow 2 end to end across three consultations; a sample-note learning run on real past notes with the review step; each style rendered for one real session; CPU seconds per prose section recorded (feeds the CUDA deferral trigger).

- **PHASE 4 ARCHITECT, LEG e12 — the encrypted-PDF refusal (2026-09-20 20:30, run=stage-4, session stage-4-arch-e12; ended `composer-run`; one source file and one test file changed; no delegation, 0 records).** DIAGNOSIS — CODE ORDER, not the fixture (both confirmed against the installed pypdf 6.19 source): `PdfReader.__init__` parses the trailer's `/Encrypt` and verifies the empty password (`_reader.py:186–196`); it does NOT raise for a user-password document — `FileNotDecryptedError` is raised from `get_object` (`:541–544`, `:580–583`) on the FIRST object access, which in `_read_pdf` was `len(reader.pages)`, and that call sat inside the same generic-translation `try` as the constructor and BEFORE the `if encrypted:` branch, so the by-name refusal was unreachable and the practitioner would have seen "not a readable PDF (FileNotDecryptedError)". The fixture WAS genuinely encrypted (the very exception proves the standard security handler; the test now asserts it: `is_encrypted`, `"/Encrypt" in trailer`, and `len(pages)` raising `FileNotDecryptedError` on a bare reader). FIX (`sample_notes.py` `_read_pdf` ~497–560): `FileNotDecryptedError` imported beside `PdfReader`; two local refusal builders (`encrypted()`, `unreadable(exc)` — the exception TYPE only, never pypdf's message); the constructor, the page count and each page's `extract_text` are three separate `try`s, each catching `FileNotDecryptedError` FIRST (→ the encrypted refusal) and everything else second (→ unreadable); `reader.is_encrypted` is checked between the constructor and the first page access, so a document the empty password DID open (an owner-only restriction) is refused by the practitioner's rule too — the message now says "encrypted (password-protected or permission-restricted)". Convention check: the filename in the message matches the `.docx` path ("could not read {name}: …"), and no message carries document text (C9). Test: `TestReadPdf::test_an_encrypted_pdf_is_refused_by_name` gains the genuineness assertions and asserts the type name is NOT in the message. VERIFIED IN-SESSION: ruff clean; mypy clean (38 files, pypdf present); `loop-history-check` OK. NOT run: pytest — `cd desktop && ../.venv/Scripts/pytest.exe -q`; expected 2734 passed, 0 failed. NEXT: composer suite → the practitioner continues the smoke (the PDF addendum in the leg-e11 bullet, then the prose-styles script) → further batches → `/document` → commit.

  APPLIED: `desktop/pyproject.toml` `pypdf==6.19.0` in BASE `dependencies` + the mypy override entry (pypdf ships `py.typed`; the override only lets mypy pass where the wheel is absent — this shell — and is inert where it is installed); `sample_notes.py` `SAMPLE_NOTE_SUFFIXES` gains `.pdf` (~93), `MAX_PDF_PAGES = 60` / `MAX_PDF_PAGE_CHARS = 20_000` with the bounds-and-residue comment (~94–114), `_read_pdf` (~467–530: pypdf imported lazily, the bytes read through ONE capped request `read(MAX_SAMPLE_NOTE_BYTES + 1)` — never `read()` — and refused over the cap, `PdfReader` over the in-memory bytes with every raise translated to the exception TYPE only, an encrypted document refused by name, the page count capped, each page's `extract_text` capped as a REFUSAL (never a silent truncation), the total bounded while collected, and a document whose pages all yield blank text refused by name with the paste hint — a scan is never OCR'd), `read_sample_note` dispatches `.pdf` and its unknown-suffix message now says ".txt, .docx or .pdf"; `ui/practitioner.py` `SAMPLE_NOTE_FILTER = "Notes (*.txt *.docx *.pdf)"` and `LEARN_INTRO_TEXT` "(.txt, .docx or .pdf)"; docs: threat-model surface 16 (the reader's bounds, refusals and the honest residue), data-flow flow 16, retention row 35, design-system's learn bullet; the plan's Excluded item struck through with the built note (OCR itself stays excluded) and Task 3.3's AS-BUILT superseded in place. THE BOUNDS, AND WHY: the byte read is this module's own cap (the `.docx` lesson: a stat is a precondition, the capped read is the allocation bound for the INPUT); pages (60) and per-page text (20 000) are a note's shape, not a book's, and a breach is a refusal because a silently truncated note would be learned from as if complete; the HONEST RESIDUE, stated in the constants comment and surface 16 and flagged here for Phase H: pypdf inflates each content stream itself and exposes no capped-read hook (unlike `ZipFile.open`), so the OUTPUT allocation is bounded only by the ≤ 2 MiB input × DEFLATE's ratio — a same-user chosen file failing with nothing written (the PR-MED-020 → LOW precedent); a bounded decode would need pypdf's filter internals, which I could not inspect without the wheel and would not guess at. TESTS: `tests/test_sample_notes.py::TestReadPdf` (9 cases; a hand-written minimal PDF writer `_pdf_bytes` — no library needed to BUILD fixtures, exact xref offsets, `Tj`/`T*` lines in Helvetica, `None` = a page with no content stream): the suffix list + picker filter + intro pins (no pypdf needed), a two-page text PDF read with the tree untouched, a scan refused with the paste hint, an encrypted PDF (written through `pypdf.PdfWriter.encrypt`) refused by name with no document text in the message, a non-PDF refused by type name only (a SECRET marker asserted absent), the page cap (61 refused, 60 read), the per-page cap as a refusal, an oversized file refused before `PdfReader` runs (monkeypatched to fail), the one capped `read(n)` request recorded through `Path.open`, and the no-pypdf refusal (`sys.modules["pypdf"] = None`); the old "scan.pdf refused by suffix" row became "photo.png"; `test_ui_learn_style.py` case 7 keeps its `scan.pdf` fixture (now refused as unreadable / as needing pypdf — the status still names the file) with its docstring corrected. VERIFIED IN-SESSION: ruff clean; mypy clean (38 files, pypdf absent here — the override); `loop-history-check` OK. NOT run: pytest — `cd desktop && ../.venv/Scripts/pytest.exe -q`; expected with pypdf installed 2724 + 9 = 2733 passed, 0 failed (without it: 2724 + 1 pass + 8 skipped — the composer must install first). WATCH ITEMS: (a) the hand-written PDF's text extraction under pypdf 6.19 — `T*` line moves should yield newlines in `extract_text`; if pypdf joins the lines with spaces instead, the "Presenting complaint" < "Plan" order assertion still holds and only a newline-based assertion would need loosening (there is none); (b) `PdfWriter.append(reader)` + `encrypt("secret")` is the pypdf ≥ 3 API — if 6.19 renamed it, the encrypted case is the one to adjust, never the reader; (c) `reader.is_encrypted` is read BEFORE `len(reader.pages)` because an encrypted page tree can raise on access. SMOKE ADDENDUM for the practitioner: after the relaunch, in "Learn from my notes" pick a Cliniko PDF export (Choose notes), tick consent, press "Learn from these notes" and read the review dialog — the headings map, the shorthand and the example sentences should be the note's own text; then Save and read the "Learned style" line; a scanned PDF should be refused with "…holds no readable text - a scanned or image-only PDF cannot be read; paste the text instead". CONSEQUENCE stated in the `/fix notes` and D2 AS-BUILT: with the drain yielding at every ready window the windows-behind rule can no longer accumulate in normal operation — fall-behind in practice is the QUEUE CAP tripping in `feed` for a provider slower than the feed (`fell_behind`, fails closed at the producer), and the windows-behind rule survives as a DEFENSIVE bound (one ingested chunk closing > 3 windows). The four regimes are written out in the PR-LOW-022 notes. - **PHASE 1 ARCHITECT, LEG b7 — PR-LOW-021 follow-up (2026-09-19 14:09, run=stage-1, session stage-1-arch-b7; ended `composer-run` — one test-file change, no source change; round 9 stays `Open (4 pending)`; no delegation, 0 records).** THE FAILURE (`test_recorder_no_sockets_during_live_transcription`, `gated.calls == 1`): a feed-timing gap in the child, not an implementation gap — `LIVE-POSTED` was announced the instant the feeder switched to the second tone and the parent's `FINISH` arrived within milliseconds, so the open span held a few 50 ms blocks, under `MIN_SPEECH_SECONDS` (0.25 s); `LiveSegmenter.finish()` dropped it at the seal by the ordinary short-span filter (`transcription.py` `_close`: `end − start < min_speech` → no segment), so the tail drain ran with no window and the provider's second call never happened. D2's tail drain is intact: an open span of ≥ 0.25 s at the seal IS closed, packed and transcribed on the worker before `drain()` returns (the `test_the_last_flushed_chunk_is_in_the_drained_tail` and `test_finish_seals_only…` cases already pin that with a 1 s tail). FIX (`tests/test_integration_no_sockets.py` `_LIVE_CAPTURE_CHILD`, the feeder + the `LIVE-POSTED` announcement): the feeder counts its second-utterance blocks and the main thread prints `LIVE-POSTED` only after 20 blocks (one second of tone, four times the minimum) have been fed; the tone stays continuous until `stop` so the span remains OPEN (a following silence would close it into a live window and move the second call before Finish). Deterministic by construction: whatever the parent's round-trip, the sealed span is ≥ 1 s. The two stdin gates are unchanged. Round-9 PR-LOW-021 `/fix notes` carry the trace.
