#### Issues Found

- **[MED]** MED-001 — `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/ui/main_window.py:206` (`session_started → transcript_screen.begin_live_view`) with `ui/transcript.py:284–294` — Start on an OPEN recovered transcript orphans its checkout and its retained unwrapped key copy
  - Classification: 🆕
  - Triage: Fix-now · Fix route: premium-only (custody ordering)
  - Why it matters: Phase 1 wired Start to `begin_live_view`, whose first act is `_clear()` (`transcript.py:542`): the recovered document's `on_complete` / `on_discard` closures — the ONLY path to `complete_recovered` / `discard_recovered` for that session — are dropped, while `MainWindow._transcript_source` still names the directory and `_recovered_crypto` (the unwrapped in-memory key, `main_window.py:109`) stays retained. Nothing on this path calls `_destroy_recovered_crypto()` or `recovery_screen.release_checkout(...)`; only `_on_live_transcript` (`:342`) and `_on_transcript_closed` (`:482`) do. If the recording is then Discarded from the Session screen (`session_discarded → clear_live_view → _clear()`), `closed` never fires, so the recovered session stays checked out (excluded from the recovery list by `protected_session_ids`) with its key copy resident and NO control able to Complete or Discard it until restart. Before Phase 1 nothing touched the transcript screen at Start; the "stays checked out until restart" residual (`:336–338`) was reached only when a live transcript actually arrived, paired with the key-copy destroy.
  - Current behaviour: Start on a recovered view drops the callbacks; Discard-during-recording leaves the checkout and the key copy orphaned. Desired: `session_started` is handled by the main window: destroy the retained key copy, release the recovered checkout (scoped, by id), clear `_transcript_source`, THEN open the live view — the same cleanup `_on_transcript_closed` performs, at the one view change that lacked it.
  - Pattern to follow: `_on_transcript_closed` (`main_window.py:475–488`). Pattern siblings: `_on_live_transcript` (`:342`) and `_on_transcript_closed` (`:482`) already pair the view change with the cleanup; `begin_live_view` was the only one that did not — none found beyond it (searched `begin_live_view`, `_clear()`, `_destroy_recovered_crypto`, `release_checkout`).
  - Invariant: after `session_started`, `MainWindow._recovered_crypto is None`, `_transcript_source is None`, and the previously recovered id is not in `recovery_screen.protected_session_ids()`, BEFORE the live header shows.
  - Verification: a `MainWindow` test opening a recovered outcome, emitting `session_started`, then asserting the invariant and that a later `session_discarded` leaves nothing checked out; the existing recovered-checkout tests (`test_ui_screens.py:2745–2810`) unchanged.
  - Regression risk: `test_ui_screens.py:2365–2385` (the live-view wiring test), `TestMainWindow` recovered-path tests.
  - /fix decision: Applied
  - /fix notes: `ui/main_window.py` — `session_started` now connects to the new `_on_session_started` (destroy the retained key copy → release the recovered checkout by id → `_transcript_source = None` → `begin_live_view`); a "live" source releases nothing. Tests: `test_ui_screens.py::TestMainWindow::test_start_releases_an_open_recovered_view_before_the_live_view_opens` (the invariant, plus a Discard during the recording leaving nothing checked out) and `test_start_over_a_live_view_releases_no_checkout`. Verified: ruff, mypy; pytest composer-run.
  - /fix date: 2026-09-25
  - /fix applied by: Claude Code

- **[MED]** MED-002 — `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/ui/note.py:1820–1828` (`_orphan_style_job`) with `ui/transcript.py:701–732` (`abandon_note_and_complete`), `:734` (`cancel_note_review`) and `:533` (Discard enabled while not generating) — session-key destruction is admitted while an orphaned prose-rendering worker still holds the note's confirmed lines
  - Classification: 🆕
  - Triage: Fix-now · Fix route: premium-only (custody residue; a bound plus a stated residue)
  - Why it matters: the transcription side fails closed — `complete()` / `complete_without_note` / `complete_deleting_saved_note` refuse an uncleared live worker (`session.py:713, 750, 779`) and `discard()` refuses while `transcribing` — but the Phase-4 prose job has no such gate: `abandon_button` is enabled whenever `_on_abandon` is set (`note.py:2210`) and `cancel_button` whenever a draft is unsaved (`:2213`), both while `_style_job` runs; Abandon → `complete_without_note(lease)` deletes the key (`transcript.py:722`) → `closed` → `note_screen.clear()` → `_orphan_style_job()`, and the worker keeps running `stage(note)` (`:1773`) — the `GeneratedNote`'s assertion texts, the `ProseInput` and the prompt inside the resident runtime live until the LAST section's model call returns. The same holds for Cancel followed by Complete/Discard on the Transcript screen. Only the close guard (`is_busy`, `:2246`) knows about orphaned jobs; no custody-destroying path does, and the threat model records only "a job orphaned by clear() keeps its thread until it reports" (surface 17), not that the key may go first. Round-24 cross-phase pair: Phase 3A's custody exits × Phase 4's worker.
  - Current behaviour: an orphaned job renders every remaining section after the key is gone; its result is dropped on arrival. Desired: (1) BOUND the residue — the tab hands every job an abort predicate that `_orphan_style_job` (and so `clear()`) flips, checked by `ProseStyleProvider.render` before EACH section's model call, so an orphaned job stops after the call in progress (at most one section's prompt and completion outlive the review — never the whole note); (2) STATE the residue where the structure is described: threat-model surface 17 and retention row 39 name "at most one section's call after Abandon / Cancel / Complete / Discard; a new Start may overlap that one call (two CPU-bound models for its duration — a C8 `fell_behind` fallback at worst, never data loss)"; the refuse-while-rendering alternative (Save's shape) is recorded as the stronger option NOT taken (it would block Abandon for a whole CPU render).
  - Pattern to follow: `render(..., only=)` already skips sections; the abort is the same skip, decided per call. Pattern siblings: `session.py:419–442` admits a new Start while an orphaned job runs (the benchmark has the symmetric guard, `microphone.py:332–342`) — the SAME residue, bounded by the same abort and named in the same sentence; none else.

- **[MED]** MED-004 — `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/config_defaults/prose_connectives.json` (`after` :11, `before` :29, `if` :62, `in` :63, `off` :77, `on` :78, `out` :81, `over` :82, `since` :103, `under` :124, `until` :125) with `note_check.py:1528` (`_POLARITY_TOKENS`) and `:1600–1607` — the connective allow-list admits antonym function words, so a polarity-REVERSING rephrase passes Check 5
  - Classification: 🆕
  - Triage: Fix-now · Fix route: premium-only (an enforcing control's contract — narrowed, never widened)
  - Why it matters: rule (a) lets any input token on the allow-list be dropped and rule (b) lets any allow-list token be added; rule (c)'s multiset covers only `_NEGATIONS ∪ {nil, n't}`. So `Off paracetamol` → `On paracetamol` passes every rule (`off` droppable, `on` addable, no negation marker, `paracetamol` count equal); so do `pain before running` ↔ `pain after running`, `refer if no improvement in 2 weeks` → `refer, no improvement in 2 weeks`, `fracture ruled out` → `fracture ruled in`, `over 2 weeks` ↔ `under 2 weeks`, `since Tuesday` ↔ `until Tuesday`. The `narrative-v2` prompt tells the model any preposition or conjunction is a free connecting word (`prose_style.py:107–110`). Phase 0's vocabulary × Phase 4's gate — the pinned disjointness test (`test_fidelity_check.py:210`) checks only the negation vocabulary.
  - Current behaviour: eleven meaning-bearing function words are free glue. Desired: those eleven leave the allow-list and become POLARITY-BEARING markers under rule (c) (a second closed set beside the negations, compared as the same multiset: neither added, dropped nor swapped); the pin extends to the new set; D6 gains an AS-BUILT addendum; the note_check docstring, surface 17's rule (c) sentence and the CHANGELOG say so; and the REAL model is re-measured on the ten fixtures in both styles (`scripts/measure-prose-fixtures.py`, granted) before the rate claim on Task 4.3 is left standing — a narrowed gate can only lower the rate, and the measured number is recorded whatever it is (docs/lessons.md 2026-09-24).
  - Pattern siblings: the remaining list was read in full — `with` (its inverse `without` is already a negation), `up` / `down`, `into` / `onto`, `through`, `across` stay (no clinical inversion found for them; named as the residue of this pass); searched `_PROSE_CONNECTIVE_SET`, `PROSE_CONNECTIVES`, `_POLARITY_TOKENS`.
  - Invariant: `_PROSE_CONNECTIVE_SET` is disjoint from every rule-(c) marker and every rule-(d) class; a rephrase that swaps `on`/`off`, `before`/`after`, `over`/`under`, `since`/`until`, `in`/`out` or drops `if` fails `polarity`.
  - Verification: `test_fidelity_check.py` class cases for each pair (fail) and a faithful rephrase that keeps them (pass); the vocabulary pin; the real-model measurement recorded on Task 4.3 and in this round's `/fix notes`.
  - Regression risk: `test_fidelity_check.py`, `test_prose_style.py` (the mock fixtures must still pass — the mock echoes the lines), `test_vocabularies_and_settings.py` (the packaged-file pins: entry count), the Task 4.3 measured rate.
  - /fix decision: Applied (as SET semantics, not the multiset the finding proposed — see notes)
  - /fix notes: the eleven words left `config_defaults/prose_connectives.json` (141 → 130); `note_check._POLARITY_BEARING_TOKENS` is the pinned closed set, kept OFF the allow-list, and they are treated as FACTS under rules (a)/(b) rather than folded into rule (c)'s multiset: the multiset shape would fail an honest merge of two lines sharing `on` ("neck pain on rotation" + "neck pain on flexion" → "neck pain on rotation and flexion"), which the real model produces; set semantics catch the antonym swap and the drop (the finding's cases) and leave two such words swapped BETWEEN facts as the already-named token-not-attachment residue (pinned as passing). Docstrings: note_check module rule (c) sentence; threat-model surface 17 rule (b) sentence; D6 addendum (9); CHANGELOG. Tests: `TestPolarityBearingWords` (6 swap/drop cases → `missing_fact`, an added one → `added_content`, a faithful keep, the merge, the residue), the vocabulary pin (`test_no_polarity_bearing_word_is_a_connective`, count 130), `test_a_changed_date_is_a_missing_fact` moved from `on` to `at`. REAL-MODEL MEASUREMENT (this leg, under the stricter multiset build): narrative 10/10 (5.2 s per section mean, 6.1 max, 10.0–19.7 s per note), own voice 10/10 (6.6 s, 7.3 max, 12.8–26.3 s) — recorded on Task 4.3 and D6.
  - /fix date: 2026-09-25
  - /fix applied by: Claude Code

On-screen text change: the status line under a disabled saved prose style now names the real reason(s) and disappears when they clear. Tests: `test_ui_writing_style.py` — the existing test computes the line through the option; NEW `test_the_fallback_names_the_disabled_options_own_reason`, `test_the_fallback_is_retracted_when_the_model_appears`; `test_ui_models.py` — `test_the_fallback_line_names_the_reasons_it_is_given`, `test_style_options_carry_their_bare_reasons`. `test_ui_screens.py:3392` is a NoteScreen (one-argument) site — unchanged.

Simpler shape: a non-Qt `NoteReviewSession` (draft, document, config, those fields, an injected clock and learning-status provider) exposing `add_line / remove_line / move_line / undo_line / edit_line`, `build_resolutions`, `refinalise() -> GeneratedNote`, `learned_rule_outcomes`, `review_state`, the edit methods returning their status text and the two "consider learning" methods their message; the widget keeps the rows, the editor request, `_rendered_excerpt` (fed in at row build — the read-back defence stays where the widget renders) and the thread plumbing. Testable offscreen without `qapp`. Two boundary items ride the same seam: `_removed: set[str]` holds both assertion ids and pre-filled PROPOSAL ids (`remove_line`), correct only because the id namespaces never collide — split or type it; `_rule_replacements: dict[str, tuple[str, str]]` where a NamedTuple exists. Behaviour-preserving by construction (a move of code behind the same public methods); pinned by `test_ui_note_editor.py`, `test_ui_prose_stage.py`, `test_ui_screens.py`. Executor recommendation: **Include in plan** (surface=production; a scoped `/review-plan` task after H5 — the ONE simplification in this round worth a task: the file is the app's largest UI unit and every later Note-tab change lands in it). Pattern siblings: none — the other screens keep their state small. Decision: recorded — the task append and its scoped hardening are the composer's
- **[MED]** SIMP-008 — the module-split family, one finding: (a) `ui/models.py` (2,470): the styles/prose stage (`:1621–2051`) and the pipeline factories + attribution (`:2052–2467`) share nothing with the rest — splittable ONLY with re-exports kept (tests and `practitioner.py` monkeypatch through `models.`); (b) `note_config.py` (2,551): learning (`:1250–2353` — the refusal filters, cues, rules) → `note_learning.py` importing the three blob helpers, and the cue sidecar (`:1599–1815`) and the rule sidecar (`:2006–2353`) are one read → validate → rules-first / sidecar-second → prune-on-read shape written twice; (c) `note.py` (2,668): `MockNoteModelProvider` (`:1639–2156`, ~520 lines of test double in product code) and the rendering block (`:761–991`); (d) `transcription.py` (2,346): the live classes (`:1378–2346`) → `live_transcription.py`, a one-directional import of the stage functions; (e) `ui/practitioner.py` (1,610): the three note-learning groups (`:1124–1466`) → a composed widget only if the attribute paths the tests reach stay on the tab; (f) `sample_notes.py` (942): the readers (`:314–632`) → `sample_notes_io.py`. Each is behaviour-preserving as a move (the pinning suites are named in round 24's inventory), none is required for correctness, and each costs a review round. Executor recommendation: **Accept** as a recorded inventory (surface=production) — take none now; revisit (a)–(d) when the next phase extends the file in question. Headless: an Accept is MUST-PAUSE — surfaced in the leg's handoff; if the composer prefers, (b)'s sidecar-shape unification is the one with the most reasoning value. Decision: recorded

- `StyleStageResult(prose_style, digest, (), 0, 0, 0, 0.0, reason)` positional at six sites — keyword form is taste.

#### Security findings
- **[MED]** SEC-001 — `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/note.py:256–264` (`content_tokens` folds case) with `config_defaults/prose_connectives.json` (`"as"`) and `config_defaults/clinical_abbreviations.json` (`"AS"`) — Check 5 compared FOLDED tokens, so the shipped abbreviation `AS` (ankylosing spondylitis) was free glue: a model that dropped or inserted the diagnosis passed every rule. Threat: the untrusted model output loses or adds a diagnosis unseen; the practitioner's reading was the only control. Trace: `["AS suspected - refer rheumatology"]` → `"Suspected, refer rheumatology."` — (a) `as` missing but a connective, (b)/(c)/(d) equal → PASSED. Mitigation applied: `as` left the allow-list AND the class is refused at IMPORT — `note_config._refuse_vocabulary_overlap(CLINICAL_ABBREVIATIONS, PROSE_CONNECTIVES)` raises `RuntimeError` ("broken install") on any connective equal to a shipped abbreviation's lower-case form, the same shape as the malformed-vocabulary guard (the lesson: confine the class, do not enumerate the instance). Pattern siblings: the whole abbreviation list folded against the connective list — only `AS` collided; `L`/`R` fold to fact tokens. Tests: `test_vocabularies_and_settings.py::test_an_abbreviation_that_folds_to_a_connective_is_a_broken_install` (the guard on a colliding pair), `test_fidelity_check.py::test_no_connective_is_a_clinical_abbreviation_folded` (the shipped pair + the two verdicts). — Triage: Fix-now; /fix decision: Applied
- **[MED]** SEC-002 — `config_defaults/prose_connectives.json` (`"up"`, `"down"`) — the same antonym class round 24 MED-004 closed, missed for this pair: `["Weight down 2 kg"]` → `"Weight up 2 kg"` passed (a droppable, b addable, (d) `{2}` equal); likewise BP, a dose, a titration. Mitigation applied: both join `_POLARITY_BEARING_TOKENS` (13 words) and leave the list (141 → 127 with SEC-001); the `down-up` case joins `TestPolarityBearingWords`; the count pin is 127. The pronoun pairs (`he/she/his/her/him`) were considered and left: a swapped sex is not a clinical inversion of a fact the note states. — Triage: Fix-now; /fix decision: Applied
- **[MED availability, fail-closed — PLAUSIBLE]** SEC-003 — `language_model.py` (`verbose=False`) with the installed `llama_cpp/_utils.py:24–58` (`suppress_stdout_stderr.__enter__`: `os.dup(1)`, `os.dup(2)`, then `dup2` to devnull, used at `llama.py:207,221` and `_internals.py:56`) — a WINDOWED process has no valid descriptors 1/2 (`desktop/pyproject.toml` ships `scribe-app` as a `gui-scripts` / `pythonw` launcher; `logging_setup` already special-cases `sys.stderr is None`), so `dup` raises `OSError` inside the runtime's constructor → `LanguageModelError("… (model load) …")` → `_LanguageModelCache._failure` remembered for the process → BOTH prose styles render `clean` with the C8 line under the documented launcher. Not clinical-safety (nothing wrong is written), but the feature would be dead on the shipped route. Mitigation applied: `language_model._give_std_fds_a_sink()` runs before the runtime is built — for each of fds 1 and 2, `os.dup` is tried; one that cannot be duplicated is given `devnull` as its sink (`os.dup2`); a console process is untouched. Tests: `test_language_model_runtime.py::TestLoadContract::test_std_fds_get_a_sink_only_when_they_cannot_be_duplicated` (injected `dup`/`dup2`), `test_the_load_gives_the_std_fds_a_sink_before_the_factory` (order). VERIFICATION OWED to the practitioner's Phase H smoke: launch `.venv\Scripts\scribe-app.exe` (NOT `python -m`), choose Narrative, finalise a note — the prose must render; if the 2026-09-24 smoke ran through the exe and Narrative rendered, the claim was wrong and the helper is a no-op (harmless). — Triage: Fix-now; /fix decision: Applied
- **[LOW availability]** SEC-004 — `prose_style.py` `_style_conditioning` (30 exemplars × up to 1 000 characters + up to 500 shorthand tokens ≈ 7–8 k tokens) against `LANGUAGE_MODEL_CONTEXT_TOKENS = 4096` — a maximal learned style makes the runtime raise on every section (`model_error`, "could not be rendered": fail-closed but useless), and a merely long prompt silently truncates the completion into a `missing_fact`. Mitigation applied: `MAX_CONDITIONING_CHARS = 6_000` — exemplars trimmed from the END of the reviewed list until the block fits, then shorthand tokens; the head (measures) always survives. Tests: `test_prose_style.py::TestConditioningBudget` (3). Residue, stated: a section whose own confirmed lines approach the window (an assertion may be 20 000 characters) still overflows — the C8 line names it. — Triage: Fix-now; /fix decision: Applied — SUPERSEDED by codex round 30 PR-MED-046 (leg h1f): the character cap became a tokenizer budget over the whole prompt (`conditioning_allowance`, `_budgeted_conditioning`, the `too_long` refusal before any call); `TestConditioningBudget` → `TestPromptBudget`
- **[MED docs]** SEC-005 — the Task 0.5 stubs (round 24 MED-007's inventory): `docs/security/threat-model.md` (the section title "Phase 0 stubs", the preamble "Only that plan's PHASE 0 is built … planned; enforced from Phase N", surface 11 "Planned; enforced from Phase 1 — none of it exists today"), `docs/security/data-flow-map.md` flow 14 ("PLANNED … no such flow exists today … Phase 0 stubs"), `docs/security/retention-schedule.md` (the title "Phases 0–3", the preamble "stubs, finalised at that plan's Phase H", row 38 "PLANNED; enforced from Phase 1" ×2). All rewritten as BUILT with the enforcing structure named (the tee `_tee_sink`, `LiveTranscriber` and its bounds `LIVE_QUEUE_CAP_BYTES` / `LIVE_MAX_SEGMENT_SECONDS` / the yielding drain, `stop()`'s `buffers_cleared` verdict and `_drop_pcm` / `_clear_buffers`, the four custody consumers that consult it and the one that may not, `finish()` sealing, the drain through `claim_live_transcriber` / `_live_transcript` / `assemble_transcript`, the C8 fallbacks, the live view's signal + `NoTextInteraction` + clear-on-Discard, the one log record) and the residue (the ≤ 0.25 s cut tail, the time-based join bounds, audio-not-window bounding). Every sibling grep (`PLANNED`, `planned; enforced`, `Phase 0 stubs`, `stubs are finalised`, `What it will`, `none of it exists`) over the three docs returns nothing. — Triage: Fix-now; /fix decision: Applied
- **[LOW docs]** SEC-006 — `docs/security/threat-model.md` surface 15 — the style store's own `ConsentRecord` carries `learning_opt_in`, written at Save and consulted by NOTHING (phrase and rule learning read the VOICE record's opt-in through `learning_status`); the surface implied the record gates learning. Now stated exactly: the version gates USE and the summary line, the opt-in is the evidence of the tick, withdrawal of a learned artefact is its deletion (the v3 text). — Triage: Fix-now; /fix decision: Applied

- Source: independent cross-family codex peer review
- Reviewer: codex gpt-6-astra (high)
- Scope: The 11 specified files and relevant plan contract; static review only, no writes or tests.
- **PR-MED-044** (MED, behavioral, `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/config_defaults/prose_connectives.json:42`): Remaining connectives still permit clinical facts to change through token insertion or deletion. `Pain before eating` → `Pain before and during eating` passes all four rules, adding a symptomatic interval; `Should have surgery` → `Has had surgery` also passes, changing a recommendation into recorded history. These are distinct from the accepted token-order residue. — Evidence: The list includes `"during"` at line 42 and `"should"` at line 92; `note_check.py:1631` exempts missing connective tokens, and `:1634` exempts added connective tokens. Neither example changes a negation or protected-token count. Recommendation: Fix-now — Narrow the remaining temporal/modal entries and add adversarial cases covering both additions and omissions; remeasure fixture acceptance. /fix decision: Applied (leg h1f, 2026-09-25) — the modals `should`/`shall`/`will`/`would` and the temporals `during`/`while`/`when`/`within`/`between` left `prose_connectives.json` (127 → 118) and joined `note_check._POLARITY_BEARING_TOKENS` (22 words; the pin `test_no_polarity_bearing_word_is_a_connective` counts 118); `TestPolarityBearingWords` gained five omission cases (the peer's `should`, plus `will`/`when`/`while`/`within`) and four insertion cases (the peer's `during`, plus `should`/`within`/`between`), each recomputed by hand from the rule order; REAL-MODEL RE-MEASUREMENT under the 118-word list (this leg): narrative 10/10 (5.0 s/section mean, 6.0 max, 9.9–19.3 s per note), own voice 10/10 (6.4 s/section mean, 7.1 max, 12.7–24.5 s per note; model load 9.3 s cold) — the narrowing cost no fixture; recorded on Task 4.3 and D6 (9). Auxiliaries stay glue (the re-tensing the tuned prompt relies on); the residue is restated in D6 (6). /fix date: 2026-09-25 · /fix applied by: Claude Code
- **PR-MED-045** (MED, behavioral, `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/language_model.py:192`): The descriptor repair closes its own repaired destination when `os.open` returns the target descriptor. For an actually closed stdout, the sink can be descriptor 1; duplicating 1 onto itself followed by closing the sink leaves stdout invalid again, so the runtime's subsequent duplication still fails. Repeated loads do not repair this case. — Evidence: `"sink = os.open(os.devnull, os.O_RDWR)"`, `"dup2(sink, fd)"`, and unconditional `"os.close(sink)"` at lines 192–196. The new test substitutes `dup2` with a recorder and never exercises this aliasing case (`test_language_model_runtime.py:319`). Recommendation: Fix-now — Retain the opened descriptor when `sink == fd`; close only a separate temporary descriptor, and cover actual closed descriptors plus repeated repair in an isolated subprocess. /fix decision: Applied (leg h1f) — `language_model._give_std_fds_a_sink` keeps the opened descriptor when it IS the target (`sink == fd`: no `dup2` onto itself, no close) and duplicates-then-closes only a DISTINCT temporary; `open_sink` (default `_open_devnull_sink`) and `close` join `dup`/`dup2` as seams; tests: the existing helper test now asserts only the distinct temporaries are closed, and NEW `test_a_sink_that_is_the_target_descriptor_is_kept_open` pins the pure aliasing shape (opened 1 then 2: no dup2, nothing closed) and the mixed shape (fd 1 aliased, fd 2 through temporary 7). The peer's subprocess check is left to the Phase H smoke through `scribe-app.exe`. /fix date: 2026-09-25 · /fix applied by: Claude Code
- **PR-MED-046** (MED, behavioral, `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/prose_style.py:261`): The new conditioning limit does not establish the claimed 4,096-token bound. It measures only conditioning characters, assumes approximately four characters per token, and reserves nothing based on the actual section length or chat framing. Token-dense conditioning or longer confirmed sections can therefore still exhaust the window and force fallback despite passing this limiter. — Evidence: `"MAX_CONDITIONING_CHARS: Final = 6_000"` at line 226; trimming uses `"while len(text) > MAX_CONDITIONING_CHARS"` at lines 261 and 264; all section texts are appended at line 296, while `output_token_budget` independently permits 768 completion tokens. `language_model.py:94` fixes the context at 4,096 tokens. Recommendation: Fix-now — Budget the complete formatted prompt with the runtime tokenizer, reserve completion space, and trim exemplars then shorthand against the remaining capacity; explicitly handle sections that cannot fit without conditioning. /fix decision: Applied (leg h1f) — `LanguageModel.count_tokens` joins the protocol (`LocalLanguageModel`: the runtime's `tokenize` with no BOS and special tokens counted, a failure typed and content-free; `MockLanguageModel`: a word count); `prose_style.conditioning_allowance` = window − (instruction + the section's user text + `output_token_budget` + `PROMPT_FRAMING_TOKENS` 64); `_budgeted_conditioning` trims exemplars from the end, then shorthand, and returns None when even the bare block does not fit; `render` refuses a section whose allowance is negative (or whose bare own-voice block does not fit) BEFORE any call as `SectionFailure` `too_long` with `SECTION_TOO_LONG_DETAIL`; `ProseResult.too_long_sections` / `errored_sections` (both kinds of "no rendering"); `StyleStageResult.too_long` and `style_stage_line` name "too long for the model's window" on the C8 line; `MAX_CONDITIONING_CHARS` removed (round 28 SEC-004 superseded); `scripts/measure-prose-fixtures.py`'s wrapper forwards `count_tokens`. Tests: `test_prose_style.py::TestPromptBudget` (6: the allowance arithmetic, exemplars-first trimming, shorthand then the bare block failing, a small style untouched, the pre-call refusal with exactly one model call, own voice rendering under the trimmed block), `test_language_model_runtime.py::test_count_tokens_is_the_runtimes_tokenizer_without_bos` (`_FakeLlama.tokenize` records the flags; a tokenizer failure names the type only). Docs: surface 17's conditioning sentence, CHANGELOG. /fix date: 2026-09-25 · /fix applied by: Claude Code

#### LEG 1 verified tuples (architect leg h1e, 2026-09-25)
- PR-MED-044: materiality=behavioral severity=med surface=production rec=Fix-now — CONFIRMED by hand from `note_check.fidelity_verdict` (rules (a)/(b) exempt any allow-list token on either side): `before` is protected but `and` + `during` are addable glue, so the interval is added unseen; `should` and `have` droppable, `has` + `had` addable, so a recommendation becomes history. The class is MODAL verbs (a recommendation or futurity turned into a fact: `should`, `shall`, `will`, `would`) and TEMPORAL prepositions/conjunctions (an interval or condition added or dropped: `during`, `while`, `when`, `within`, `between`) — nine words, the MED-004 / SEC-002 recipe: leave the list (127 → 118), join `_POLARITY_BEARING_TOKENS`' pin, add the peer's two cases plus one per word as `TestPolarityBearingWords` addition/omission cases, and RE-MEASURE the real model (`scripts/measure-prose-fixtures.py`, granted) before the rate claim stands — if a fixture drops, the drop-out is named as residue, the list is NOT widened back (the practitioner's rule). The auxiliaries (`has`/`had`/`is`/`was`…) stay: protecting them removes the re-tensing glue the tuned prompt relies on (`test_an_input_connective_may_be_dropped_or_retensed`) and the re-measurement would decide against it; the honest limit is restated in D6's residue (6): no finite allow-list closes "an added or dropped connective shifts tense or adds an interval" — the practitioner's reading is the control.
- PR-MED-045: materiality=behavioral severity=med surface=production rec=Fix-now — CONFIRMED at `language_model.py:186–197`: when fd 1 (or 2) is closed and a LOWER descriptor is open, `os.open(os.devnull)` returns fd 1 itself; `dup2(1, 1)` is a no-op (POSIX) or a self-close-then-dup (the Windows CRT), and the unconditional `os.close(sink)` closes fd 1 again — the runtime's `os.dup(1)` then fails exactly as SEC-003 set out to prevent. Under the pure `pythonw` shape all three std descriptors are invalid so `os.open` returns 0 and the code happens to work — the bug is the shape where a parent closed only stdout/stderr. Fix: when `sink == fd` keep it (it IS the sink) and record it; close the temporary only when it differs; give the helper `open_sink`/`close` seams beside `dup`/`dup2` and pin the aliasing case (the sink returned equals the target: no close of the target) — an in-process test, no subprocess needed (the peer's subprocess suggestion is optional evidence for the smoke).
- PR-MED-046: materiality=behavioral severity=low surface=production rec=Fix-now — CONFIRMED that `MAX_CONDITIONING_CHARS` bounds only the conditioning block in characters (`prose_style.py:226, 261–266`) and reserves nothing for the section's own lines (up to `MAX_ASSERTION_CHARS` = 20 000 each) or the completion (`output_token_budget` ≤ 768); severity LOW because the failure is fail-closed (`model_error`, "could not be rendered") and needs a near-maximal learned style or an outsized section. Decision between the two shapes: the TOKENIZER budget — the runtime already exposes `Llama.tokenize` (no new dependency; D8/C1 hold), so `LanguageModel` gains `count_tokens(text) -> int` (`LocalLanguageModel` via the runtime's tokenizer, `MockLanguageModel` by a whitespace count) and `render` budgets the WHOLE formatted prompt: window − the completion reservation (`output_token_budget`) − a fixed chat-framing margin − the user text (title, markers, lines, instruction) = the allowance for the conditioning block, trimmed exemplars-then-shorthand against it; a section whose lines alone exceed the window is refused BEFORE the call with a named C8 reason ("too long for the model's window"), never a runtime raise. The character heuristic was rejected: an honest 3-chars-per-token margin would cut own-voice conditioning to ~3 exemplars — the feature — while still not bounding the lines. `MAX_CONDITIONING_CHARS` goes (a token budget replaces it); D6/surface 17/row 41 restate the bound.
- Cap verdict: raise +1 — production-behavioral — three confirmed MEDs (two as MED, one verified LOW) need code fixes and a confirmation round; the pass is at 5/5. (FOLLOWED at the composer seat: cap 5 → 6; leg h1f applied all three — decisions: PR-MED-044 Applied, PR-MED-045 Applied, PR-MED-046 Applied.)

What the tuning changed: (1) the EXACT-FORM rule with worked tokens ("doesn't stays doesn't, nil stays nil, 2 stays 2, 12/03 stays 12/03, HVLA stays HVLA") and "never spell a number out"; (2) the framing words the untuned model kept adding named as forbidden ("the patient", "presents", "noted", "performed", "applied", "given", "prescribed") plus "a line with no verb stays without one"; (3) a worked example whose CONTENT words come from no fixture's facts (elbow ache / heat; the function word `typing` in its second line recurs in one fixture line, "Wrist pain typing" — codex round 23 PR-LOW-043) showing lines joined as sentences with a verb-less treatment line kept verb-less — the two treatment failures that remained after (1)–(2) ("Soft tissue work to the lumbar paraspinals" → "… was performed on …") closed on that example alone; (4) the own-voice person text no longer suggests "the patient" as the third-person subject; (5) the prompt kept SHORT because the runtime's state is cleared after every call (PR-MED-034), so the whole system prompt is re-evaluated per section on the CPU host — the same rules with the 141 connectives listed inline reached 10/10 narrative but at 9.5 s/section (27–38 s per note), a long rule set 9/10 at 6.6 s; the allow-list is therefore DESCRIBED by category, not listed. Candidate widening words the practitioner allowed (`patient`, `there`, `presents`, `presented`, `lasted`, `today`) were NOT needed and NOT admitted: `patient` and `pain` are pinned as non-connectives by `test_vocabularies_and_settings.py` (Phase 0's reasoning — a subject word changes what a sentence claims), and the tuned prompt avoids them. WHAT A TUNED PROMPT STILL CANNOT DO (the honest residue of a token gate over a 4B model): the passing prose is the lines restated as sentences in their own words ("Neck pain for three days, worse on turning left." / "Continue paracetamol. Review 12/03.") — a fluent narrative rewrite ("The patient presents with…") is exactly what rule (b) refuses, so "Narrative" reads as joined clinical sentences, not as dictated prose; a 30-exemplar own-voice profile adds ~400 prompt tokens per section (the fixture profile has 2), so the practitioner's Task P.2 measurement on the REAL profile is the number that decides the CUDA trigger, not this fixture's 6.1 s; and the repeat-and-obey instruction shape passes (PR-LOW-037). Speed is within the plan's bound for narrative and at its edge for own voice
